Inder J. Taneja RGMIA Research Report Collection, 20(2017), pp.1-75, http://rgmia.org/v20.php
Patterns in Prime Numbers:
Fixed Digits Repetitions
Inder J. T aneja1
Abstract
In this paper, we shall work with prime numbers in such a way that they can be extended by
inserting digits in repeated ways, and still it continues prime numbers. These types of prime numbers
called ”Patterns in Prime Numbers” . The repetition of digits are with one, two, three, four, ﬁve and six
digits. This work is from 10 to 7 string length 7, i.e., in each case we have 10 to 7 primes numbers with
repeated digits.
The work of this paper is divided in sections and subsections given below:
1 Introduction;
1.1 Multiple Prime Patterns ;
2 Patterns with Strings of Length 10 ;
2.1 Single Digit Repetitions ;
2.2 Double Digits Repetitions ;
2.3 Triple Digits Repetitions ;
2.4 Four Digits Repetitions ;
3 Patterns with Strings of Length 9 ;
3.1 Single Digit Repetitions ;
3.2 T wo Digits Repetitions;
3.3 Three Digits Repetitions ;
3.4 Four Digits Repetitions ;
4 Patterns with Strings of Length 8 ;
4.1 Single Digit Repetitions ;
4.2 T wo Digits Repetitions;
4.3 Three Digits Repetitions ;
4.4 Four Digits Repetitions ;
4.5 Five Digits Repetitions ;
5 Patterns with Strings of Length 7 ;
5.1 Single Digit Repetitions ;
5.2 Double Digits Repetitions ;
5.3 Triple Digits Repetitions ;
5.4 Four Digits Repetitions ;
5.5 Five Digits Repetitions ;
5.6 Six Digits Repetitions .
1Formerly, Professor of Mathematics, Universidade Federal de Santa Catarina, 88.040-900 Florian´ opolis, SC, Brazil.
E-mail: ijtaneja@gmail.com; Web-site: www.numbersmagic.wordpress.com.

1 Introduction
First let us understand what we mean by ”patterns in prime numbers” . It is understood as repetition
of same digits in limited continuous way the the resulting number still remains as prime numbers. See
some examples,
• 71
701
7001
70001
700001/period.math
• 11
116141
1161416141
11614161416141
116141614161416141/period.math
• 1069
101869
10181869
1018181869
101818181869
10181818181869/period.math
• 47
41357
41351357
41351351357
41351351351357
41351351351351357/period.math
Let us analyse the above prime numbers. In the ﬁrst case digit 0 repeats 4 times. In the second case
the the digit 1614 repeats 4 times. In the third and forth examples, the numbers 18 and 135 repeats 5
times respectively. Also to note that in each case the next number in the string are not prime numbers.
See below:
700001 = 197× 35533;
1161416141614161416141 = 82549× 14069415033666809;
1018181818181869 = 11× 92561983471079;
41351351351351351357 = 29× 9461× 597643× 252181271/period.math
Let us examine two interesting examples (refer [2]),
• 31
331
3331
33331
333331
3333331
33333331/period.math
• 59
599
59999
599999
59999999
59999999999
59999999999999/period.math
The diﬀerence between two examples is that the ﬁrst is uniform in increasing single digit 3 to next.
In the ﬁrst case the string can start at any place. The next example in this case is not a prime,
333333331 = 17× 19607843/period.math
The second example is not uniform. In this case the next of similar time prime number is
59999999999999999999999/period.math
2

--- page 3 ---
Inder J. Taneja RGMIA Research Report Collection, 20(2017), pp.1-75, http://rgmia.org/v20.php
Our study is limited to the examples of ﬁrst kind, i.e., uniform patterns. This is divided in sections
and subsections according to number of primes in each category. This work is limited to 7 to 10 strings
of primes, but is written below in reverse order i.e., from 10 to 7. Thus in each case, single, double,
triple, ... digits repetitions are done separately. In each case few examples are given. By no means,
these only examples, there might be much more.
1.1 Multiple Prime Patterns
Below is a example of a prime number 50487611, where the pattern of lenght 9 is formed by three
diﬀerent ways, by digits 489, 894 and 948 respectively.
▶ 50 487611
50 489 487611
50 489 489 487611
50 489 489 489 487611
50 489 489 489 489 487611
50 489 489 489 489 489 487611
50 489 489 489 489 489 489 487611
50 489 489 489 489 489 489 489 487611
50 489 489 489 489 489 489 489 489 487611/period.math
▶ 504 87611
504 894 87611
504 894 894 87611
504 894 894 894 87611
504 894 894 894 894 87611
504 894 894 894 894 894 87611
504 894 894 894 894 894 894 87611
504 894 894 894 894 894 894 894 87611
504 894 894 894 894 894 894 894 894 87611/period.math
▶ 5048 7611
5048 948 7611
5048 948 948 7611
5048 948 948 948 7611
5048 948 948 948 948 7611
5048 948 948 948 948 948 7611
5048 948 948 948 948 948 948 7611
5048 948 948 948 948 948 948 948 7611
5048 948 948 948 948 948 948 948 948 7611/period.math
We observe that in all the 3 cases the primes are the same in each line, for example, the last one in
each case is 50489489489489489489489489487611. Only diﬀerence is in way the digits for repetition
are choosed. This is due the fact that 48 is appearing in the prime as well as in the repetition digit:
5048(948)7611. For simplicity, let us call the as ”multiple pattern prime numbers”. The detailed study
on this type of prime numbers shall be dealt elsewhere.

--- page 4 ---
Inder J. Taneja RGMIA Research Report Collection, 20(2017), pp.1-75, http://rgmia.org/v20.php
For study on numbers in diﬀerent situations can be seen in author’s work [6]. This work is summary of
author’s on numbers work for the past few years. See also author’s recent work [7] on Hardy-Ramanujan
number 1729. This work brings the number 1729 in diﬀerent situations. From historical point of view,
refer [1, 2, 4, 5].
2 Patterns with Strings of Length 10
In this section, we shall give examples of patterns with diﬀerent digits repetitions for primes length 10.
This has be divided according digits in the subsections below.

3 Patterns with Strings of Length 9
In this section, we shall give examples of patterns with diﬀerent digits repetitions for primes length 9.
This has be divided according digits in the subsections below.
3.1 Single Digit Repetitions
Below are few examples, with simpliﬁed notations.
8

4 3 3 3 3 7881
4 3 3 3 3 3 7881
4 3 3 3 3 3 3 7881
4 3 3 3 3 3 3 3 7881
4 3 3 3 3 3 3 3 3 7881/period.math
Above two patterns we have written in two diﬀerent ways. Left side is simpliﬁed notation, while
right side is complete patterns. Instead writing whole the pattern, we can write is int reduced form
representing the same way. The left side is understood as
[A/comma.math B; /k.math]
where A is prime numbers, B is prime number with repetition of digits. The letter /k.math represents the total
prime numbers. In the above two cases, k=9 . Thus, above two examples can equivalently be written as,
• [6073/comma.math 69073; 9]
• [47881/comma.math 437881; 9]/period.math
From now onwards, we shall use frequently, these noations. Below are more examples,

4 Patterns with Strings of Length 8
In this section, we shall give examples of patterns with diﬀerent digits repetitions for primes length 8.
This has be divided according digits in the subsections below.
4.1 Single Digit Repetitions
Below are few examples, with simpliﬁed notation.

5 Patterns with Strings of Length 7
In this section, we shall give examples of patterns with diﬀerent digits repetitions for primes length 7. This has
be divided according digits in the subsections below.
5.1 Single Digit Repetitions
First example given above can be represented two diﬀerent ways. See below:
• [31/comma.math 331; 7] ▶ 31
3 31
3 3 31
3 3 3 31
3 3 3 3 31
3 3 3 3 3 31
3 3 3 3 3 3 31/period.math
• [31/comma.math 331; 7] ▶ 31
3 3 1
3 3 3 31
3 3 3 3 31
3 3 3 3 3 31
3 3 3 3 3 3 31
3 3 3 3 3 3 3 31/period.math
• [3557/comma.math 35507; 7] ▶ 3557
355 0 7
355 0 0 7
355 0 0 0 7
